Instead, they were chosen through a multi-step process involving several layers of representation. Here's a simplified breakdown of the process:\n\n- **Primary Assemblies**:  \nEligible citizens (active citizens, who were male, at least 25 years old, and met certain property or tax requirements) gathered in primary assemblies in their communes to elect electors. These electors would then represent the commune in the next stage.\n\n- **Electoral Assemblies**:  \nThe electors chosen in the primary assemblies would then gather in electoral assemblies at the department level. Here, they would elect two sets of representatives: the departmental electors and the members of the Council of Ancients and the Council of Five Hundred (the two chambers of the legislative branch).\n\n- **Departmental Electors**:  \nThe departmental electors would then meet in the capital of their department to elect the members of the Senate and the Consuls. The Senate was responsible for proposing a list of candidates for the Consulate.\n\n- **Consular Election**:  \nThe Council of Ancients would then select three candidates from the list proposed by the Senate. The Council of Five Hundred would vote on these three candidates, and the one receiving the most votes would become the First Consul. The other two candidates would become the Second and Third Consuls, respectively.\n\nThis system was designed to ensure a balance of power and prevent any single individual or group from gaining too much influence. However, it was also complex.
